## October 23 — filtered fruit-bat sample and split-topology audit

**Question:** can the published Egyptian-fruit-bat context task be reconstructed safely enough to justify a first local ML baseline?

**Check:** extended the aggregate-only annotation audit using the paper's stated filter: exclude Unknown and Landing contexts, require a positive emitter ID, and retain intervals no longer than 750,000 samples at 250 kHz. The checksum-verified local `Annotations.csv` yields 35,074 rows across 34,603 FileIDs, matching the paper's reported filtered count. The script exports counts only and keeps `training_admitted` false. It does not compare the exact retained rows or folds. A graph connecting source files and positive known emitter/addressee IDs has nine components; the largest contains 33,516 rows (95.56%), 33,048 files and 35 participants. This indicates that a balanced split holding all those entities together is not available from the current annotation graph. It does not establish that the published folds are wrong, and unknown/ambiguous roles or unjoined sessions, dates, channels, treatments and chambers could change the grouping.

**Validation and boundary:** the synthetic filter/grouping regression test passes; the aggregate report contains no sample FileIDs or participant IDs. No audio, `FileInfo.csv`, model code, weights, split files or predictions were acquired or run. This is a metadata-topology diagnostic only; it does not open training readiness. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Next decision:** ask an independent bat-bioacoustics/behavior and ML-evaluation reviewer to select the estimand and say whether exact fold/retained-row metadata or a bounded session join is necessary. Do not train, fetch audio or acquire the 31.6 MB FileInfo file until that review establishes the smallest useful, rights-cleared check.

## October 23 — BirdSet benchmark and rights review

**Question:** can a large bird-audio database provide a credible first machine-learning step for Talk2Nature?

**Finding:** Rauch et al.'s BirdSet is a substantial avian sound-classification benchmark: the paper reports over 6,800 hours and nearly 10,000 training classes, with eight strongly labeled evaluation datasets totaling over 400 hours. The current card lists the XCL snapshot at 528,434 recordings / 484 GB and recommends an HSN regional configuration at 5,460 training recordings / 10,296 test labels / 21 classes / 5.92 GB. The card dates its Xeno-Canto snapshot to 2024-03-10. These figures describe the upstream benchmark, not Talk2Nature results. Its labels address species and acoustic events; they do not establish animal comprehension or meaning.

**Rights and implementation:** the dataset card carries a collection-level CC BY-NC 4.0 / non-commercial research-and-education restriction, while individual recordings carry varied licenses. The May 2026 maintainer thread documents a license-metadata correction and remaining format inconsistency. The relationship between the umbrella restriction and each component license remains unresolved. The new research page and separate paper/dataset resource records therefore remain metadata-only. No article body, audio, labels, code or weights were acquired or run. No training admission or commercial use is approved. The card also has conflicting dataset-version compatibility notes and its live viewer cannot load the custom dataset script; exact revision and location-field handling need review before any future acquisition.

**Project decision:** retain BirdSet as a methods reference for an acoustic perception layer, not as the first communication benchmark and not as a parrot-context dataset. The current data-led communication-adjacent ML candidate remains the separately gated Egyptian fruit-bat context question; the possible budgerigar cohort remains a distinct community-observation path. Any bird benchmark should first have a defined task and component-level rights review. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Validation:** all 145 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 116 pages plus 404 and passed the site checker across 117 HTML pages / 5,754 links. The explicit-origin public candidate also produced 116 pages plus 404 and passed the checker across 117 HTML pages / 5,871 links / 116 reachable public pages. The new research page was visually inspected at 390 px and 320 px viewport widths; no horizontal overflow or broken layout was observed. `git diff --check` passed. This is a local candidate, not a deployment or independent specialist review.

## October 23 — Google live fetch check for the sitemap

**Question:** is Search Console's current “Couldn't fetch” status explained by a broken or blocked sitemap on the live site?

**Search Console evidence:** in the verified `https://metivity.github.io/talk2nature/` property, the submitted `/sitemap.xml` row remains `Unknown` / “Couldn't fetch” with zero discovered pages or videos. Its detail page shows “Sitemap could not be read” and a last-read date of October 23, but no more specific error. URL Inspection's live test for the exact sitemap reports “URL is available to Google”; its Page availability details say crawl allowed `Yes`, page fetch `Successful`, and indexing allowed `Yes`, crawled as Google Inspection Tool smartphone at 04:34 on October 23. Discovery is “Not checked in live tests.” This verifies a successful Google live fetch, not scheduled sitemap processing, discovery, or indexing of the listed pages.

**Independent live checks:** the sitemap returned HTTP 200 with `application/xml`, parsed as the standard namespaced `urlset`, and contained 115 unique absolute HTTPS URLs, all under the expected GitHub Pages origin. `robots.txt` returned HTTP 200, allows `/`, and declares the same sitemap. With Python's missing default CA bundle corrected by pointing its HTTPS verifier at the installed `certifi` bundle, `scripts/check_live_site.py --attempts 1` verified the homepage, app, resource directory, 115 sitemap URLs and robots declaration.

**Acceptance checks:** all 145 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 115 pages plus 404 and passed the site checker across 116 HTML pages and 5,704 links. The explicit-origin public build passed across 116 HTML pages, 5,820 links and all 115 public pages reachable. `git diff --check` passed.

**Decision and limits:** no site-code change or repeat submission is justified by this evidence. Leave the existing request in place and recheck after Google's processing/retry window; if the report still fails, repeat the live check and inspect any new, specific error before changing the site. Google's [Sitemaps report guidance](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7451001) says the report shows the latest submitted-request status, a fetch failure can have transient causes, and a live URL Inspection test is the way to confirm crawl permission and page fetch. The live check does not prove Google has processed the sitemap or indexed its 115 URLs. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**; this SEO verification does not change any biological or data-readiness gate.

## October 23 — fail closed on placeholder split identities

**Question:** can the research manifest splitter distinguish a missing animal/session key from a valid pseudonymous ID before it creates grouped partitions?

**Finding and change:** the current validator already rejected `unknown`, `na` and `n/a` for animal IDs, and it used a broader placeholder set for optional grouping dimensions. It still accepted `none`, `null`, `tbd` and `pending` as animal IDs, and accepted any nonempty placeholder as a session ID. These values could silently merge unrelated rows under a shared key. Reused the same placeholder set for animal IDs and now reject placeholder session IDs. Added regression coverage and documented the current-main behavior in the researcher guide, while clarifying that the older exact-output pin predates the stricter check.

**Provenance and limits:** this change uses only the committed synthetic manifests and tests; no third-party recordings or metadata were acquired or inspected. It verifies declared strings only. It cannot establish that pseudonyms represent distinct animals/sessions, discover omitted relationships, validate rights or labels, or make any dataset benchmark-ready. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Validation:** all 146 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 116 pages plus 404; the site checker passed 117 HTML files and 5,754 links. `git diff --check` passed. This is a local source change, not a deployment, independent reproduction or real-data validation.

**Next decision:** apply this current-main validator to any future clean-source rehearsal, but do not admit a real manifest or run a model until the source rights, label provenance, independent reviewer and full grouping policy are resolved.

## October 23 — WABAD candidate added to the research library

**Finding:** the 2026 WABAD paper reports 5,047 minutes of audio and 91,931 species-level vocalization annotations from 1,192 bird species, 72 sites, 29 recording locations and 13 biomes. The latest Zenodo v4 record lists 21.3 GB. This is a promising scale and geographic-coverage reference for bird-call detection and site-held-out evaluation, but the reviewed description does not establish behavior, receiver response or call meaning.

**Rights and limits:** the publisher abstract says the dataset is CC BY 4.0, while the current Zenodo v4 record says CC BY-NC 4.0. This remains unresolved. The full article methods, supporting metadata, archive contents, split, source-recording provenance and per-file terms were not inspected. The article and current dataset version are now separately documented in the source/resource catalog and a new source-linked research page. They remain metadata-only; no recording, annotation, code or model was acquired or run.

**Decision:** consider WABAD only for a narrowly defined bird-detection task after the rights discrepancy, data schema, grouping structure and labels are reviewed by an independent bioacoustics specialist. Do not treat it as a communication benchmark or commercial-ready corpus. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Validation and release:** all 146 Python repository tests and 115 Node tests passed. The public-origin build produced 117 pages plus 404; the site checker validated 118 HTML pages and 5,919 links; `git diff --check` passed. The new note was inspected at desktop and 390 px mobile viewport widths. PR #214 was merged as `540308763909f44e45418c87f06ed610901b76cf`; GitHub Pages run `39890054141` completed successfully, including live-route and crawl-metadata verification. A TLS-verified request returned HTTP 200; the route has an indexable robots directive and canonical URL and appears in the live sitemap. No physical-phone check, archive analysis or independent reviewer check was performed.

## October 23 — make the advanced journal export identifiable to assistive technology

**Question and evidence:** can a keyboard/screen-reader user find the optional journal-only export after completing the microphone-free practice? On the deployed `/app/station/?mode=demo`, the synthetic task started, accepted a `Movement` note, stopped, and produced a partial recap labelled invented audio, local-only, with one note. In the browser accessibility tree, the visible “Save journal only · JSON” control appeared as an unnamed button; a role query by its visible label could not find it.

**Change:** added a matching explicit `aria-label` to the export button and a generated-page regression check that its accessible label stays aligned with its visible text. The control remains inside the optional “Advanced · research export” disclosure.

**Validation:** all 147 Python and 115 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 117 pages plus 404; the site checker validated 118 HTML pages and 5,800 links. The explicit-origin public candidate also produced 117 pages plus 404; its checker validated 118 HTML pages, 5,919 links and all 117 public routes. The required Android checks also passed: core boundary tests, debug lint, debug/release assembly, and release-boundary checks. In the rebuilt browser flow, the disclosure opened and the export was discoverable by its accessible name and reported visible and enabled. The practice remained synthetic and local; no microphone or export/download was used.

**Release:** PR [#217](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/217) merged to `main` as [`14481f4`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/14481f4423c3939577a708c6fbc0402df53a7dcc). Public checks [39894405770](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/39894405770) passed. Pages run [39894591870](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/39894591870) completed successfully, including the live-route and crawl-metadata verifier.

**Live verification:** `/app/station/` returned HTTP 200, and the deployed export button carries `aria-label="Save journal only · JSON"`, matching its visible text. Android core, lint, debug/release assembly and release-boundary checks also passed as required by the repository workflow.

**Limits and next decision:** this is browser accessibility-query evidence, not VoiceOver, TalkBack, physical-phone or independent novice testing. Run the screen-reader path on iOS and Android before claiming assistive-technology validation. No recording or download was made during verification. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 23 — candidate species and large-data review

Reviewed the OpenWhistle bottlenose-dolphin preprint, its live Hugging Face pretraining/classification cards and file inventories, and the separate model-card license statement. The authors report about 114 hours from five known dolphins over five years and 8,354 expert-labeled whistles. Published tasks detect whistle presence or classify whistle types; the paper explicitly says this does not establish semantic meaning. Its evaluation split is session-level, not a whole-individual test.

**Decision:** document OpenWhistle as a strong new research-benchmark candidate, while retaining companion parrots as the first app-participation scenario. The article states CC BY 4.0 for OpenWhistle, but the dataset cards omit license fields and the separate model card says its license is unspecified. No data or model files were acquired or run; component rights and independent specialist review remain gates. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**. No code, app or deployment change was made.

## October 23 — reconcile the benchmark run card with OpenWhistle

**Question:** does the candidate run card distinguish the newly cataloged dolphin benchmark from the bat-context question, bird detection and parrot identity work?

**Evidence reviewed:** OpenWhistle arXiv v1 (2609.34839; accepted as a NeurIPS 2026 Datasets & Evaluations Spotlight), its official project benchmark page, and the current Hugging Face pretraining, classification and model cards. The public Hugging Face metadata APIs returned immutable revisions `eea37423c7841b0f99a7c0380f945999c0a2c489` (pretraining), `2b7806e3c5913c459c40b824ab7f944d494b3993` (classification) and `985ac11bc6939af5e41383143aad5a0ae6993f68` (model). The cards describe 114.284 hours of unlabeled 96 kHz audio, 8,354 expert-labeled whistles in ten categories, and session-disjoint train/validation/test partitions. The five-year corpus covers one site and five known dolphins; signature-whistle labels identify named individuals. The paper reports whistle detection/classification, not social-context or receiver-response evaluation.

**Rights check:** the arXiv paper states CC BY 4.0. The exact pinned Hugging Face API metadata returned no machine-readable license value for either dataset card or the model card; the model card says its terms have not been specified. Keep the rights of paper, audio, labels, code and model separate. Public and ungated access does not resolve permission. No file bytes or model weights were acquired or run.

**Change and decision:** added Candidate D to `docs/BENCHMARK_CANDIDATE_RUN_CARD.md`, with exact source revisions, task boundary, session-vs-individual generalization limits, separate rights gates and a proposed reproduction path. Updated the research note's review locator and pinned the paper, dataset-card and model-card source records to the exact versions checked. Retained Egyptian-fruit-bat context prediction as the current fit for the communication-adjacent context question. OpenWhistle is now a distinct alternative for within-pod acoustic-structure reproduction; it is not evidence of meaning and does not replace the bat task or parrot app cohort.

**Validation and limits:** Hugging Face revision endpoints returned the exact pinned commits and `cardData.license: null` for all three snapshots. All 147 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 118 pages plus 404; the site checker validated 119 HTML documents and 5,851 links. The explicit Pages-origin build also produced 118 pages plus 404; its checker validated 119 HTML documents, 5,970 links and 118 reachable public pages. A local browser content/link inspection confirmed the rendered note shows the source revisions, review depth, rights boundary and no-translation limits; this was not a mobile-layout or assistive-technology review. Both research/source JSON files parsed, and `git diff --check` passed. No dataset, model, code or audio was acquired or run; no reviewer was contacted; no deployment had occurred when this candidate review was recorded. This documentation reconciles the candidate map but does not select a benchmark as ready. Independent cetacean and ML review, component-level reuse terms, leakage-aware temporal analysis and the existing AutoResearch readiness gates remain open. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 23 — Google reports sitemap unreadable while live URL is indexable

**Question:** is Google Search Console's no-index result for the OpenWhistle research note caused by a live page/crawl problem, or by discovery/report processing?

**Evidence:** inspected Search Console on the Talk2Nature URL-prefix property. The indexed-state report says the OpenWhistle URL is not on Google and unknown to Google, with no referring sitemap or page. The read-only live URL test completed at 9:13 AM on October 23, 2026 and says the URL is available to Google and can be indexed. The Sitemaps report lists `/sitemap.xml` as submitted and last read on October 23, but labels it “couldn't fetch” and reports zero discovered pages. Separately, the live sitemap responds HTTP 200 with `application/xml`; `robots.txt` responds HTTP 200 with `text/plain`; the repository's TLS-verified live-site checker validated three core routes, all 118 sitemap URLs and the robots-to-sitemap link. The sitemap report date and inspected URL are from the UI; the underlying Google fetch failure was not explained there.

**Decision and limits:** no site or sitemap code change is indicated by the independent live checks. The sitemap is already listed as submitted, so no repeat submission or URL-indexing request was made. The Search Console evidence is specific to this property and inspection time; it does not establish general Google discovery, ranking or search traffic. Recheck after Google has had time to process the existing sitemap, and keep the SEO outcome unresolved until Search Console reports a successful sitemap read and discovers the URL. The direct local Python verifier first failed because the host Python CA store could not verify the certificate; rerunning with the installed `certifi` CA bundle preserved TLS verification and passed. No certificate verification was disabled.

## October 23 — monk-parakeet social-structure research note

**Finding:** the 2025 paper reports social networks for 337 monk parakeets, relatedness for 100, and 5,599 vocalizations from 229 birds over two years. The 2023 voiceprint paper reports the same call/bird totals, so this is an extension of an overlapping cohort, not independent acoustic replication. Social measures are associated with call variation; the study does not test receiver responses or decode call meanings.

**Change and rights:** added the research note, three separate paper/data/code resource records, and a parrot reading-path link. The paper declares CC BY 4.0; Zenodo v1 declares MIT at record level but its 129.5 MB archive remains uninspected; the pinned code tree has no located license and its README notes missing inputs and UTM/nest-location fields. No audio, archive, code or model was acquired or run. The note contains no coordinates and remains unmapped pending sensitivity review. A share-ready, evidence-bounded post is in `docs/NETWORK_AND_OUTREACH.md`; no outreach was sent.

**Release:** PR [#231](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/231) merged as [`2fafda2`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/2fafda2a86ff97d1d05721785c8d37ac2315ba34). The first Pages deployment [39913610112](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/39913610112) passed its build, deploy and live-route verifier. Live inspection found a duplicated year in the publication line; PR [#232](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/232) corrected it as [`a603ecd`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/a603ecdfe188d56e1e0d53261569fc766b1e8043). ## October 23 follow-up — Google can fetch the sitemap, but discovery remains unresolved

**Question:** is the Search Console “Sitemap could not be read” result explained by a current site or robots failure, or is it a Google report/processing discrepancy?

**Evidence:** on the Talk2Nature URL-prefix property, the sitemap report still says “Sitemap could not be read,” last read 10/23/26, with zero discovered pages. A live URL Inspection test of the exact sitemap, completed 11:21 Israel time on October 23, reports “URL is available to Google”; Crawl allowed = Yes, Page fetch = Successful, Indexing allowed = Yes, and Crawled as Google Inspection Tool smartphone. The live test says discovery was not checked. The Manual actions report says “No issues detected.” The Page indexing report is last updated 10/16/26 and lists five indexed examples (resources, starting-points, research directory, mobile page and homepage; last crawls Oct 14, 12, 11, 11 and Sep 30). Its summary reports zero non-indexed pages while its table lists one “Crawled - currently not indexed” row; opening that reason reports zero affected pages. Treat these indexing counts as a stale/internally inconsistent snapshot, not a current inventory.

Independent live checks used TLS verification with the installed certifi CA bundle after the host's default CA path failed. `scripts/check_live_site.py` then verified the homepage, app and resource directory, parsed the sitemap and checked the robots directive. A separate bounded fetch of every sitemap location found 120 URLs and 120 HTTP 200 responses with no redirects. `/sitemap.xml` returned HTTP 200 and `application/xml`; `/robots.txt` returned HTTP 200 and names the exact sitemap without a site-wide disallow.

**Decision and limits:** no sitemap/site code change, repeat submission or URL-indexing request is indicated by this evidence. The current live test establishes present accessibility, not a successful scheduled sitemap read, URL discovery, indexing, ranking or traffic. Google says it retries transient sitemap fetch failures and advises resubmission only when the problem persists; see the [Sitemaps report guidance](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7451001?hl=en-GB). Recheck after Google's next processing cycle and retain the indexing outcome as unresolved in the meantime.

## October 23 — zebra-finch dataset candidate screened

**Question:** is there a usable public dataset with enough ground truth to begin a testable animal-communication research task?

**Evidence reviewed:** the V33DA dataset card and file listing at pinned Hugging Face revision `975ddf36a1f0c0bffc512bf2c2e6aa40bb2a1913`. The card describes 33,625 caller-attributed events from 10 individually identified zebra finches, across three experiments and 16 recording days, with synchronized audio, video, pose and telemetry. It says caller truth was derived from on-body accelerometers and lists CC BY 4.0. The provider reports 18.8 GB. The dataset card marks its associated manuscript as under review. No files, code or models were acquired, inspected or run.

**Decision:** add V33DA as a metadata-only candidate for one bounded technical task: attribute an already-detected, isolated call to a visible bird, excluding accelerometer channels from model inputs and retaining them only as oracle truth. This can test caller attribution, not call meaning, behavioral context, receiver understanding or conversation. An unseen-bird split is essential; the card's held-out-experiment regime changes bird identity, group, date and cage together, so it cannot identify which shift caused any performance change.

**Limits and next gate:** the 33,625 events are nested within only 10 animals and one aviary/recording geometry; overlapping calls are excluded from the main benchmark. CC BY 4.0 is the repository's declaration, not an independent audit of each audio/video component or a legal opinion about commercial reuse. Verify component notices, rights, group identifiers and frozen split membership before acquisition or training. No biological result, independent reproduction or project model result is claimed. The mobile app remains passive-only; no playback or treatment experiment is authorized by this dataset review.

## October 23 follow-up — choose a dataset by the question it can answer

**Evidence reviewed:** at the pinned V33DA code commit `e988b985e38909b40b2705cdad994e3d9307181e`, the top-level LICENSE is MIT for software and `data/v33da_sample/README.md` separately declares CC BY 4.0 for its 100-event real-media sample (~60 MB as described by the README). The official split-preparation script assigns recordings by experiment/date; its held-out-experiment mapping puts all BP05 dates in test. The repository README advertises leave-one-bird-out evaluation, but the referenced split implementation and exact fold membership were not independently verified. BP05 has 5,829 events across three birds, including one with only 56 events. No code, sample or model was acquired or run.

**New comparison:** the bioRxiv v3 ZF-AIM abstract reports more than 1,000 hours of female zebra-finch vocal interactions and AI-driven interaction experiments. Its linked Zenodo record lists a 26.6 GB bundle and says code and model weights are MIT; the reviewed record does not clearly specify the data/recording license or inventory. Added separate metadata-only library entries for the study and release. This is the closest newly found lead for exchange timing, but it remains a preprint and no playback experiment is part of Talk2Nature's passive app.

**Decision:** keep the three task lanes separate: Egyptian-fruit-bat context prediction remains the leading communication-adjacent behavior task; V33DA is the clearest small, ground-truth-rich caller-attribution candidate; parrots remain the provisional companion-app discovery species. ZF-AIM is a priority literature/data-rights lead, not yet a reusable dataset. Broad multi-taxon soundscape resources such as El Silencio are useful for detection and identification but lack independent behavioral/receiver-response labels for the project's central communication question. Updated the first-animal plan and source/resource library. No sample intake, training, outreach or playback occurred.

**Next gate:** the V33DA run card is now drafted. Inspect its pinned sample manifest through source metadata only and prepare the physical-bird split diagram for independent avian and ML review; do not fetch the full 18.8 GB release. For ZF-AIM, obtain an explicit component-level data license and inspect the release manifest before considering any reuse. No current candidate establishes animal-language translation.

## October 23 — Search Console indexing and sitemap follow-up

**Question:** is the sitemap problem a broken public artifact, and can Google fetch the updated research directory?

**Current Search Console evidence:** the Page indexing report was last updated October 16. Its summary showed five indexed URLs and zero not-indexed URLs/no reasons, while the reason table separately showed one “Crawled — currently not indexed”; opening that row showed zero affected pages. Treat these as a stale and internally inconsistent report, not a reconciled URL count. The sitemap detail showed last read October 23, zero discovered pages and “Sitemap could not be read”; it exposed no more specific cause in the reviewed view.

**Page-level check:** URL Inspection reports `/research/resources/` as indexed, with a Googlebot smartphone crawl on October 14, successful fetch, crawl/indexing allowed, and the user-declared canonical matching that URL. No referring sitemap was detected. A live URL test at 3:08 PM on October 23 said the page is available to Google and can be indexed. Search Console then confirmed that a recrawl request was added to its priority queue. This is a request receipt, not evidence that Google has since recrawled, refreshed its stored content or changed search results.

**Independent public-end check:** verified-TLS requests returned HTTP 200 for both `robots.txt` (`text/plain`) and `sitemap.xml` (`application/xml`). `robots.txt` names the exact sitemap URL. The sitemap parsed as the standard sitemap `urlset` and contained 120 unique URLs, all under the property prefix. The public build checker had already confirmed every public route was reachable. Google documents sitemap submission as a crawl hint, not a guarantee, and lists temporary processing errors among possible causes ([Sitemaps report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7451001), [Build and submit a sitemap](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/sitemaps/build-sitemap)).

**Decision:** no sitemap or robots change is justified by the evidence; their current public responses and structure pass. One page-level recrawl request was submitted for the updated resource directory; no repeated sitemap submission was made. Recheck Search Console after processing time. If the sitemap error persists, use any newly exposed error detail to select one targeted fix or one resubmission. Search indexing counts, impressions and rankings remain unverified beyond the page-level status above.

## October 23 — bat auditory-cortex vocal-production study

**Question:** what does a recent bat-neuroscience paper add to the project’s research map, and is its archive a candidate for our first audio benchmark?

**Evidence reviewed:** the full main text of Babl, Röhrig and Hechavarría (2026) through PMC, including sample, synchronized neural/acoustic methods, category definitions, main analyses, discussion, data/code statement and article license; and the public GIN DOI landing page. The paper reports ten awake *Carollia perspicillata* bats, 32 sessions, 631 selected vocal events and 396 neuronal units. Neural activity was associated with call class and sequence complexity around the animals’ own vocal production. Pre-vocal activity tracked social-call syllable count; the echolocation pulse-count relationship appeared after vocal onset. The reported SVM used randomized call-event-by-neuron instances; the reviewed methods do not establish held-out-animal or held-out-session performance. This is self-production neurophysiology, not receiver comprehension or a call-meaning result.

**Provenance and limits:** the paper is CC BY 4.0. The GIN record lists a 5.8 GiB archive containing neural and acoustic data and declares CC BY-SA 4.0 at repository level. We did not inspect the archive manifest, file members, code, dependencies or supplementary files. Those rights and components remain separate. No recording, archive, code or model was acquired, executed or reproduced; no independent specialist reviewed the Talk2Nature assessment. This is comparative neuroscience material, not a practical phone-capture design or a candidate for the first audio/context benchmark.

**Change:** added one source-grounded research note, separate paper/data/code resource records and source records 368–369; added this reading to `docs/RESEARCH_BACKLOG.md`. All records remain metadata-only. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Validation:** all 154 Python repository tests passed. The production-origin build generated 124 public pages plus the 404 page; `scripts/check_site.py` found 125 HTML documents, 6,336 links and 124 reachable public pages. The new route has its canonical URL, `index,follow`, a unique description and a sitemap entry. Desktop and 390-pixel responsive browser previews showed the summary, study table and collapsed review-depth disclosure without horizontal clipping; the temporary viewport override was reset. `git diff --check` passed. These checks establish content/build behavior only, not external scientific review or Google indexing.

## October 23 — budgerigar edge-monitoring prior art

**Question:** has a long-running dedicated listening device already been used to monitor parrot vocal activity, and what would remain distinctive for Talk2Nature?

**Evidence reviewed:** Agishev et al., *Edge-Based Long-Term Automated Monitoring of Budgerigar Vocal Activity Using YAMNet*, *Animals* 16(20), 3246, DOI 10.3390/ani16203246. Reviewed the publisher early-access record and abstract only; the publisher's full-page request was rate-limited and the record says complete PDF/HTML/XML versions will follow. The abstract reports ten budgerigars monitored under stable housing from June 2025 to May 2026, local Raspberry Pi processing with YAMNet features and a task-specific binary classifier, and a one-second vocal-activity measure. Detailed validation, false-positive rates, ethics, data availability, code and model terms remain unreviewed.

**Decision and rights:** continuous edge-based vocal-activity monitoring is prior art, so Talk2Nature should not present a listening station or activity counter alone as novel. The study does not, from the abstract reviewed, establish caller identity, context, receiver response or call meaning. The publisher displays an Open Access label, but the exact article license and all data/code/model rights remain unresolved. No files, audio or weights were acquired or run. Added one abstract-level evidence note and separate paper resource; the device/app cohort remains distinct from the current ML benchmark candidates.

## October 23 — V33DA++ overlap and context companion review

**Question and finding:** can a publicly cataloged, larger zebra-finch release support a concrete first ML task? The pinned V33DA++ Hugging Face card reports 3,784 overlapping-call rows, 33,625 padded single-vocalizer events, and 3,784 overlap-padded events. The last bucket repeats the overlap events with context; the provider's 41,193-row total therefore is not a unique-event count. The card lists 167 GB. The recordings and ten individually identified birds are the same as V33DA, so the row count does not increase the independent animal sample.

**Decision and boundaries:** catalogue it as a metadata-only candidate for vocal activity, overlap handling, caller attribution, source separation or audio-visual timing. The card does not supply independent behavior, receiver response or meaning labels; more context is not evidence of conversation. Its dataset card declares CC BY 4.0, but the associated code, preprocessing tools, manuscript and weights have separate terms. Read-only inspection of the pinned public code snapshot also found that its trainer imports absent `v33da.data` modules and its split script does not produce the advertised leave-one-bird-out folds. It is not currently reproducible from that snapshot. No media, code, sample, weights or archive were acquired or run; no model result or independent review is claimed. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 23 — choose a first dataset by scientific objective

**Comparison reviewed:** the Great Tit Song Dataset paper describes 109,963 annotated songs, over 1.1 million acoustic units and more than 400 birds; its value is population-scale song structure, not receiver response or meaning. The ZF-AIM v3 preprint abstract reports over 1,000 hours of female zebra-finch vocal interactions and AI-mediated playback experiments. Its linked Zenodo record lists 26.6 GB and states MIT for code and weights, but the data/recording license is unclear; methods and archive inventory remain unreviewed. These are two different scientific opportunities, not a single interchangeable “big dataset.”

**Decision:** propose Great Tit as the first bounded, population-scale audio-methods exercise, after exact OSF files, rights/privacy scope and a non-duplicative question are checked. Prioritize full methods and component-rights review of ZF-AIM as the more communication-relevant turn-taking lead. Keep V33DA++ for overlap/activity engineering; it uses the same ten zebra finches as V33DA and is not an independent animal cohort. Keep Kiki/parrot app work in the product/usability lane. No recordings, code, weights or data archives were acquired or executed, no model or playback was run, and no acquisition or training is authorized by this comparison. The sequence is recorded in `docs/FIRST_ANIMAL_PLAN.md`.

## October 23 — ZF-AIM full-text review and first-species decision

Reviewed the full main text of the bioRxiv v3 zebra-finch ZF-AIM study, including its interaction and model methods, results, discussion, and version-specific data-availability statement. It reports more than 1,000 hours of female vocal interactions and analyzes 834,309 calls. A response is defined as the first call within one second after a partner call ends. The paper reports acoustic response/covariation and task-specific next-caller, next-token and wait-time prediction; this is evidence about contingent acoustic patterns, not translated semantic content. The report of 20 birds in one Results passage conflicts with 21 in Methods. Evaluation is described by pair, but bird-level disjointness is not established in the reviewed paper.

The official Zenodo record lists a 26.6 GB archive, a record-level MIT field, and a description that states code and weights are MIT. Its exact file manifest and audio/benchmark-data rights remain unresolved. The archive and supplements were not inspected or acquired; no model was run. The preprint's release statement and later Zenodo record are version/timing-specific and not reconciled. Added a source-linked research note, updated its metadata-only resource record, and refreshed the first-animal plan and backlog.

**Recommendation:** continue treating Great Tit as the population-scale song-structure methods candidate, zebra-finch ZF-AIM as the most direct turn-taking literature lead, Egyptian fruit bat as a blocked context-prediction candidate pending group/split review, and parrots as the separate phone-app feasibility cohort. There is no single “largest dataset” that supports all these tasks. Do not start by training a general animal-language model.

## October 24 — separate a large-data starting point from a communication study

**Question:** is there a bird and public database where Talk2Nature can begin applying machine learning without overstating what the data show?

**Evidence reviewed:** reviewed the full methods of the 2025 great-tit song-culture paper and the authors' Wytham dataset documentation, alongside the October 23 OSF API manifest already recorded in `research/wytham_osf_manifest_2026-10-23.json`. The source reports 109,963 annotated songs, 1,161,033 acoustic units and more than 400 birds. The 2025 paper trained a ViT metric-learning model over the corpus and used embeddings for repertoire similarity and demographic culture analyses. The dataset labels song structure and repertoire, not independently observed interaction context or receiver response. The OSF node declares CC BY 4.0; its manifest contains nestbox metadata and a perimeter shapefile whose values and location sensitivity remain uninspected. Separately, the ZF-AIM data deposit has conflicting CC BY-SA and CC BY-NC declarations and does not yet establish full corpus coverage.

**Decision:** Great Tits are the strongest reviewed *large-data methods* starting point, but only for a scoped reproduction or a clearly distinct acoustic-structure question after independent review; generic embeddings, similarity or culture analysis would duplicate published work. Zebra finches are closer to a controlled exchange question, but ZF-AIM remains metadata-only until data terms, corpus scope and bird-level split independence are resolved. Budgerigars remain a separate possible household-app cohort, not a substitute public benchmark. No current candidate licenses a claim of decoded call meaning.

**Limits and next gate:** no Wytham payload was downloaded, no model was trained, no outside reviewer was contacted and no animal study was conducted. First review the exact proposed task, split and potentially sensitive metadata with independent avian-bioacoustics and evaluation specialists. Preserve the existing synthetic-only private intake gate; AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 24 — zebra-finch call-category perception study added

**Question:** is there a tractable existing bird study that connects machine-readable sound labels with a measured animal discrimination task?

**Evidence reviewed:** the full PMC-indexed text of Elie et al., Science 389 (2025), the UC Berkeley paper-copy record, the Figshare audio-library page and API metadata, Zenodo version metadata/API responses, and the authors' public repository README and root listing. The paper reports trained discrimination of 11 researcher-defined call types by 12 adult zebra finches, using more than 8,000 renditions from 45 vocalizers; call-type confusions were related to the researchers' behavioral-use categories beyond the acoustic-similarity comparison. The paper's interpretation about mental representation of meaning remains an inference from discrimination behavior.

**Change:** added a source-linked research note and separate paper, behavioral-data/code, and audio-library records. Updated the first-animal comparison to distinguish this behavior-linked zebra-finch lead from the larger Wytham song-structure corpus and from the parrot app cohort.

The PMC paper and Berkeley copy cite different identifiers. The exact version used for the final published analyses, archive contents and audio-to-trial joins remain unresolved; no media, archive or code was acquired or run.

## October 24 — inventory the global passerine repository without acquiring its data

**Question:** does the prominent 2026 global birdsong analysis expose a concrete, rights-traceable sound corpus that Talk2Nature can build from?

**Evidence reviewed:** the paper's PubMed abstract and author repository README, the pinned GitHub recursive-tree metadata at commit `1629e665f902cad405ff2f7bf2a0e9dc8c09b47e`, the GBIF Xeno-canto metadata record and two unrelated Xeno-canto recording pages. The paper reports acoustic structure across more than 3,000 passerine species. The README describes 116,792 song bouts and eight motifs but cites the placeholder archive DOI `10.5281/zenodo.XXXXXXX`.

**Inventory and decision:** the non-truncated API tree lists 1,456 tracked files, including 1,280 audio-segment paths totaling 185,758,300 provider-reported bytes, a 12,796,164-byte metadata CSV and a 79,543,989-byte traits CSV. These counts are repository file metadata, not a verified number of unique recordings; no payload checksums were recomputed. The tree has no standalone license/copying file, while the README states CC BY 4.0 without resolving the scope across author code, derived tables and third-party recordings. GBIF's CC BY-NC 4.0 metadata declaration is not a blanket audio license; Xeno-canto terms vary by recording. Exact filename-to-recording joins and item-level study-recording terms remain unverified. Save the bounded snapshot at `research/global_passerine_repo_tree_2026-10-24.json` and keep the source metadata-only, not admitted to training or a benchmark. It is strong acoustic-structure prior art, not evidence of receiver comprehension or call meaning.

**Recommendation and next gate:** for a communication-focused research lead, zebra finch/ZF-AIM is more directly relevant because it studies contingent vocal exchanges, but remains a preprint with unresolved data-license/corpus scope and bird-level split questions. For a useful phone product, Kiki or one parrot household may test usability, not species-wide behavior or communication. Keep these lanes separate. Before any content retrieval from the passerine repository, determine whether path metadata plus permitted public record metadata can establish exact source IDs and licenses without retaining sensitive coordinates; otherwise stop and select a cleaner source. Obtain independent avian-bioacoustics and evaluation review before a benchmark or model run. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 24 — human-guided zebra-finch clustering research note

**Decision:** zebra finch is a practical first *methods cohort* because published work gives us a bounded way to start: organize similar-sounding clips with model suggestions and human correction. This is not a conclusion that zebra finches are the best animal for every study, nor that their calls have been translated.

**Evidence and change:** added a source-grounded research page, six separate resource records (paper, paper-cited Zenodo v1 and v2, a same-title but distinct Zenodo record, pinned MIT code and a WhisperSeg checkpoint), a focused independent-review brief, and updates to the first-animal comparison, model/data decision log and backlog. The article reports 1,454 calls from three isolated birds; human-refined clusters scored near manual expert agreement on a within-bird vocalization split and took less reported annotation time. That split does not establish generalization to unseen birds, and acoustic clusters are not validated behavior or meaning.

**Rights and limits:** all records remain metadata-only; no archive, code or model was downloaded or run. The article, each data record, code and weights have separate rights. Zenodo API license metadata does not settle the external source-audio subset acknowledged by the paper; the model page/API license values disagree. No independent reviewer was contacted and no Talk2Nature result was reproduced.

## October 24 — cross-species F0 dataset reviewed and catalogued

**Question:** is a large existing animal-vocalization dataset a useful first machine-learning resource for Talk2Nature, and what would it actually let us measure?

**Evidence reviewed:** full PMC article text; the official Dryad landing page, file inventory and access description; Dryad's reuse guide; and the paper-linked GitHub tree at pinned commit `b57cc78f5b1c42b4eb61bdc4dc0f4430211c62ea`. The article reports 250,670 calls across 14 taxa. The Dryad release lists 13 taxa with waveforms and F0 annotations, plus dolphin predictions without dolphin waveforms; the listed archive totals 27.86 GB. Rodents account for 224,705 calls (about 89.6%) and monk parakeets for 233. The article's target-trained CREPE pitch-accuracy value for monk parakeets is 0.30 under its specified contour metric. It is pitch estimation, not call-meaning or receiver-understanding accuracy.

**Decision:** add an original public research note and four component-specific metadata records for the article, Dryad data, repository code and model weights. Dryad states its deposited datasets are CC0 and recommends scholarly citation; this review did not reconcile every original recording source. The article page showed no explicit open license, and the pinned 75-entry code tree has no LICENSE/COPYING file; code and weights remain unresolved. The task is a possible low-level feature benchmark only. It does not displace the provisional budgerigar household-app path or controlled zebra-finch methods candidates. No audio archive, code or model was acquired or run; no data was admitted to storage or training.

## October 24 — correct the zebra-finch benchmark novelty boundary

Do not describe that page as indexed, and do not repeat the request because Google says repeat submissions do not improve queue position.

**Sitemap diagnosis:** Search Console still lists `/sitemap.xml` as `Couldn't fetch` / `Sitemap could not be read`, with zero discovered URLs. Its URL Inspection live test for the exact sitemap reports it is available, crawl allowed, and page fetch successful. A current public fetch returned HTTP 200 with `application/xml`; the XML parser found 134 unique URLs and the InsectSet459 route; `robots.txt` returned HTTP 200. These checks show the published file is accessible and well-formed, but do not prove Google has processed the sitemap. No sitemap code change or repeated submission is justified by the current evidence.

**Decision and limits:** retain the sitemap, keep the new page's indexing state pending, and wait for Google to recrawl and refresh its aggregate reports. With only 56 impressions and no query rows, there is not enough search evidence to make keyword-specific copy changes. Search Console notes that live-test availability is not an indexing guarantee and that URL Inspection can be newer than aggregate indexing reports ([URL Inspection guidance](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9012289), [Page indexing guidance](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7440203)). This snapshot verifies crawl and performance reporting only, not ranking, audience fit, or scientific use.

## October 24 — refresh funding fit and readiness gates

**Question:** which currently listed routes merit preparation, and what must be known before an application or public campaign is responsible?

**Evidence reviewed:** the official [IIA Pre-Seed page](https://innovationisrael.org.il/programs/preseed/) and current Track 7/open-source policy records; Experiment's [FAQ](https://experiment.com/faq), [researcher payout guide](https://experiment.com/guide/extra) and existing campaign-creation guide; the current opportunity register for BiodivFuture, the Kunming Biodiversity Fund, the Coller Dolittle Prize and National Geographic calls.

**Finding and decision:** Pre-Seed remains the only near-term conditional institutional financing lead identified, with a November 9, 2026 noon Israel-time deadline and hard company, finance, R&D allocation and investor gates. The IIA procedure webpage associates open-source rules with Pre-Seed, while its linked PDF identifies Track 1; Track 7 applicability and treatment of the project's component licenses remain unresolved. Experiment supports reviewed research campaigns and all-or-nothing funding, charges fees if funded, and says launched campaigns cannot be deleted. Its FAQ's bank-country list and researcher guide's non-US payout-pilot instructions differ; Israeli eligibility remains unknown. No campaign should launch before that route, research question, budget, endorser and initial audience are established.

**Change:** refreshed source/opportunity dates and detail; added explicit payout-policy and fee links; added an ignored owner-only readiness worksheet. Updated the active goal with the next factual gates. Applicant entity, investor, finances, written IIA clarification, payout acceptance, scientific host and campaign audience all remain unverified.

The generated opportunities page contains the current cutoff, decision gates and payout links. The owner checklist is ignored by Git and absent from `dist/`.

**Limits:** this is desk research and a local readiness checklist, not legal or financial advice. No funder was contacted; no applicant declarations, application, crowdfunding page or funding commitment was made. The private checklist is ignored and excluded from public builds. The broad five-track project goal remains active.

## October 24 — tighten the first-animal recommendation around Wytham access and location data

**Question:** does the Wytham Great Tit corpus remain a useful large-data starting point after checking what is actually shared and what the metadata can reveal?

**Evidence reviewed:** the dataset authors' [nestbox metadata guide](https://nilomr.github.io/great-tit-hits/site/metadata/nestboxes/) says British National Grid X/Y coordinates are present in `nestboxes.csv` and also `main.csv`; their [pipeline guide](https://nilomr.github.io/great-tit-hits/site/pipeline/) describes the prepared song-data workflow, recommends an HPC cluster for computationally intensive steps, and states the original approximately 9 TB continuous soundscapes are not provided. No coordinate values, audio, data tables or code were accessed in this update.

**Decision and change:** retain Wytham as a prepared-song methods candidate, not a communication/meaning dataset. Any task must add something beyond the 2025 metric-learning, repertoire-similarity and song-culture work. The prepared files cannot support a claim that Talk2Nature reproduced detection from the full original soundscapes. Keep precise nestbox data and derived identifiers out of public outputs unless a qualified wildlife-location review establishes safe need. Synchronized `docs/FIRST_DATASET_DECISION.md`, the active Wytham gate in `docs/GOAL.md`, and the public first-animal guide; added regression assertions for data availability and location caveats.

## October 24 — checksum-verified aggregate check of the moth/plant source table

**Question:** can one narrowly permitted public behavioral dataset support a transparent, reproducible source-table check, and does it change the first-animal benchmark decision?

**Evidence and acquisition:** rechecked the official [Mendeley Data v1 record](https://data.mendeley.com/datasets/yg7ms8rn37/1), which declares CC0 1.0, then used the bounded collector to fetch only the 51,160-byte `data.xlsx`. The receipt verifies that its SHA-256 matches the value displayed in the file panel. The other 31 listed files and separate eLife source-data ZIP remain unacquired. No API token, deposited code, spreadsheet formula, or model was used.

**Change and finding:** added `research/inspect_moth_oviposition_sample.py`, an aggregate-only standard-library XLSX audit, a JSON result, and synthetic tests. It groups balanced side records by experiment, season, repetition, date and arena. All five main [Table 1](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12768406/) tuples match for repetitions, paired observations and egg-cluster sums: wet/dry (17, 17, 53); 60/min playback (38, 45, 67); deafened females (23, 23, 39); healthy plants with playback (29, 39, 110); and males (19, 29, 48). The result is a source-table consistency check, not a reproduction of the paper's hierarchical models or adjusted contrasts.

The workbook also contains a 2/min group (10, 20, 32) and a separate “Bat” group (14, 18, 42). The reviewed paper mentions bat playback in a flight-response validation, not a reported bat-playback oviposition result. The Bat rows remain unmapped and excluded from interpretation pending source clarification. Updated the existing moth research note and catalog entry; no duplicate research page was added. The sample remains in ignored local storage and is not in the website, Git, examples, private database or training data.

**Validation:** 165 local Python tests, including the acquired workbook, and 117 Node tests passed; the research catalog validates 212 records. The local noindex build produced 134 pages plus 404 and passed the checker at 135 HTML files/6,839 links. The production-origin candidate passed at 135 HTML files/6,975 links with all 134 public routes reachable. CI validates synthetic XLSX parser fixtures and the committed aggregate report because the source workbook is intentionally not bundled; the real-file integration test runs when the pinned sample is present in ignored local storage. The browser preview showed the updated note and source caveat; the responsive preview reached 480 CSS pixels (the tool's minimum effective width) with no horizontal overflow and visible mobile navigation. This is browser emulation, not physical-phone testing.

**Limits and next decision:** the dataset is a useful reproducibility example, not a candidate animal-audio or communication benchmark, and does not change the first-animal review decision. No inferential reproduction or independent specialist review occurred; no reviewer was contacted. Continue the independent bioacoustics/statistical review gate before considering any wider reproduction. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 24 companion-animal cohort comparison

**Question:** do existing cat, dog or parrot resources justify choosing one as the first household app or ML cohort?

**Evidence reviewed:** consolidated the already cataloged CatMeows record, full dog-bark classification paper, 2022 and 2026 companion-parrot survey reviews, budgerigar vocal/sociality deposit and 2026 edge-monitoring abstract. This is a library-bounded synthesis, not a systematic search. No media, data files, survey workbooks, code or model weights were newly acquired or run.

**Finding and decision:** the reviewed records do not supply one public household corpus that combines natural audio, independently timed behavior or receiver-response labels, verified grouping keys and reuse terms suitable for this project. CatMeows contains 440 sounds from 21 cats, with elicited contexts and CC BY-NC 4.0 terms. The dog study reports 16,789 segments from 74 dogs under designed home exposures; its dog-held-out evaluation does not establish household/session/stimulus independence, and its paper describes access by request. Parrot surveys are caregiver reports, not audio corpora. Budgerigar social/vocal files are not event-aligned source audio, and long-term activity detection is not conversation analysis. Added the research-library page source `companion-animal-cohort-evidence-2026` and the comparison to `docs/FIRST_DATASET_DECISION.md`.

The comparison is not a systematic review, independent science review, or permission to collect real observations.

## October 24 — complete the synthetic Station-to-Compare browser handoff

**Question:** does a synthetic session survive the complete local export/import path and remain understandable on a narrow screen?

**Method and result:** at 390 × 844 and 320 × 720 CSS-pixel browser viewports, started a user-triggered synthetic Station session, added a human-entered movement note, stopped it at 6.5 seconds, and saved the generated ZIP. Compare reopened that exact ZIP from a local file chooser. Its summary showed one retained recording, one observation, a partial whole-moment window, observer-declared “Not sure yet,” and soundscape context as “not recorded,” not absent. The expanded view preserved the note at 3.4 seconds and clip bounds of 0.0–6.5 seconds. The page explicitly said the file was opened locally and recordings play only after pressing Play; the audio element remained paused with no source attached before manual playback. At both widths, document width equaled viewport width and no horizontal overflow was present.

**Provenance and limits:** this used invented practice tones generated by Talk2Nature, not animal audio. The ZIP was downloaded locally and reopened locally; nothing was uploaded or automatically saved. No microphone permission was requested. Responsive emulation and a successful file round trip do not prove physical iPhone/Android behavior, screen-reader accessibility, independent novice success or research validity. No Android device was connected and `adb` is unavailable in this environment. Keep physical-device installation, download and reopen testing open; the scientific benchmark remains **NOT READY**.

## October 24 — refresh Horizon Europe call status and fit

**Question:** has the EU biodiversity-monitoring call opened, and does its current scope create a realistic Talk2Nature funding path?

**Evidence reviewed:** the official [EU Funding & Tenders call listing](https://ec.europa.eu/info/funding-tenders/opportunities/portal/screen/opportunities/calls-for-proposals?callIdentifier=HORIZON-NATURE-2027-02&isExactMatch=true&order=DESC&pageNumber=1&pageSize=50&status=31094501%2C31094502%2C31094503) now shows HORIZON-NATURE-2027-02-05 as **Open For Submission**, opening October 13, 2026 and due January 14, 2027 at 17:00 Brussels time. The direct topic-detail page still rendered a generic JavaScript shell in this review, so the submission state is attributed to the call listing. Rechecked the official Horizon Europe [Part 14 work programme](https://research-and-innovation.ec.europa.eu/document/download/01964d0c-a8eb-4f23-baa4-87d96812dca6_en), pages 13–15 and 144–147: the topic is a €16 million RIA expecting three projects at €5–6 million each. Proposals must address one policy area—the EU Pollinators Monitoring Scheme, soil biodiversity under the Soil Monitoring Law, or Water Framework Directive monitoring—and include demonstrations, scalable monitoring, identification/quantification, relevant genomic methods and area-specific DNA libraries, with FAIR-by-design data. General Annex B supplies the standard three-independent-entity, three-country consortium rule, including at least one EU Member State.

**Decision:** this corrects the earlier “portal status unverified” entry to **open**, but it does not improve fit. Talk2Nature is presently a passive audio observation/annotation project and has no validated method for the call’s legally defined monitoring and identification needs, nor a confirmed applicant entity, scientific lead or consortium. Do not pursue it as a lead application or distort the project scope. Consider only a clearly bounded software/interoperability role if a qualified eligible consortium demonstrates a concrete need for a tested Talk2Nature component. No account registration, application, outreach or commitment was made.

**Change and next gate:** refreshed the public opportunity record and its official sources. Keep the opportunity on watch, but prioritize independent scientific review and an outside researcher’s reproducibility attempt over speculative grant writing. A future funding pass should focus on calls directly suited to open research infrastructure or a qualified species-monitoring study; applicant and host facts remain unresolved. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Validation:** all 166 repository Python tests passed, with one intentional skip for an external sample not bundled in CI. The public-origin candidate built 135 pages plus 404; `scripts/check_site.py` validated 136 HTML documents, 7,054 links and all 135 public routes. This is local candidate evidence only until the reviewed GitHub change and Pages deployment complete.

## October 24 — reconcile the zebra-finch paper's cited data version

**Question:** does the published zebra-finch call-perception paper's data statement identify the same Zenodo archive as the later resubmission, and what remains unknown before a reproduction?

**Evidence:** the full PMC-indexed Science article links its Data and materials statement directly to [Zenodo record 13623969](https://zenodo.org/records/13623969). A TLS-verified read of the official Zenodo API identifies 13623969 as version 1.0, created for the manuscript at its August 31, 2024 submission, with a 30,929,664-byte ZIP and a record-level CC BY 4.0 declaration. The same concept DOI (10.5281/zenodo.13623968) has a later July 2025 version 3.0 at [record 15795568](https://zenodo.org/records/15795568), described as a resubmission after reviewer comments, with a 99,341,119-byte ZIP and the same record-level license declaration. The UC Berkeley institutional copy cites the concept DOI rather than the versioned DOI in the PMC article. The paper separately points to the [Figshare acoustic-call archive](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.11905533.v1).

**Decision and change:** the journal article's citation pointer is now clear: it names the initial-submission record, while a later concept-DOI version exists. That does not establish which exact released files generated the final published analyses, whether v3 supersedes or changes those analysis artifacts, or how behavioral records join to the separate audio archive. Updated the first-animal plan and active goal to preserve this distinction. The candidate is a controlled, trained call-category discrimination study; it does not establish spontaneous conversation or a general translation system. Keep the archives metadata-only and the benchmark unselected pending artifact-level review and independent avian plus ML/statistical assessment.

## October 25 — recheck Pre-Seed funding fit and open-source scope

**Question:** does the approaching Israel Innovation Authority Pre-Seed cutoff offer a credible, compatible route for this open-source project?

**Evidence reviewed:** the current official [Pre-Seed page](https://innovationisrael.org.il/programs/preseed/), the Authority's [open-source procedure index](https://innovationisrael.org.il/rules/%D7%A0%D7%95%D7%94%D7%9C-%D7%A7%D7%95%D7%93-%D7%A4%D7%AA%D7%95%D7%97/), its linked [open-source procedure PDF](https://innovationisrael.org.il/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/%D7%A0%D7%95%D7%94%D7%9C-%D7%A7%D7%95%D7%93-%D7%A4%D7%AA%D7%95%D7%97.pdf), and the current [Track 7 rules dated July 15, 2026](https://innovationisrael.org.il/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/%D7%9E%D7%A1%D7%9C%D7%95%D7%9C-%D7%94%D7%98%D7%91%D7%94-%D7%9E%D7%A1-7-%D7%A7%D7%A8%D7%9F-%D7%94%D7%94%D7%96%D7%A0%D7%A7-15.07.2026.pdf). The Pre-Seed page lists November 9, 2026 at 12:00 Israel time and requires an Israeli-incorporated commercial-R&D company, no more than NIS 3 million in prior funding and NIS 300,000 in prior-calendar-year revenue, a signed recognized-investment commitment of at least 15%, a round up to NIS 6 million and at least 75% for R&D. The procedure index associates the policy with Pre-Seed; the linked PDF identifies itself as Annex 6 to Track 1. A text search of the current Track 7 PDF found no express open-source provision; that absence does not settle scope.

**Decision and change:** mark the opportunity not ready to apply. The project record does not confirm the legal applicant, its financing/revenue status, a qualifying investor commitment or a complete R&D/IP plan. Do not rely on an open-source exception or restriction until the Authority clarifies scope in writing and counsel reviews project-specific assets. Refreshed source records 27, 158 and 159 and the Pre-Seed opportunity; updated the regression test to pin the date, unmet eligibility and open-source uncertainty.

**Validation and limits:** all 170 Python tests passed (one intentional skip); the explicit-origin public build produced 138 pages plus 404, and the site checker verified 139 HTML pages, 7,229 links and 138 reachable public routes. This is a dated desk-readiness screen, not an eligibility/legal conclusion; no applicant facts were invented, no IIA contact was made, and no application was drafted or submitted. The local candidate has not been deployed.

## October 25 — complete a zero-command save-and-reopen rehearsal

**Question:** can a visitor preserve a moment with plain-language controls and reopen the saved work without handling JSON or extracting a ZIP?

**Evidence:** in a fresh local noindex build, opened the companion home at the narrowest available emulated viewport (480 × 844 CSS pixels). The document width matched the viewport and the observation cards used one column. From `/app/`, the no-microphone link opened the rehearsal setup; pressing its in-console “Try without a microphone” control started the synthetic session. The default remained a 30-second whole moment with the animal unknown. No microphone prompt appeared. Added one human “Movement” note, stopped at 4.8 seconds, and received a partial recap labeled invented practice audio. “Save session” generated a ZIP in Downloads. Opening that ZIP in Compare showed “Invented example,” a partial whole moment, 4.8 seconds observed, one retained recording, one observation, unknown animal identity and soundscape context “Not recorded.” The app kept the file local and stated that playback only occurs when requested; no playback was started.

**Decision and limits:** the local candidate completes the synthetic save/reopen loop without JSON handling or manual archive extraction. This advances the maintainer-run zero-command check, not independent usability or field readiness. It does not test a physical phone, real microphone capture, iOS/Android file handling, screen readers or a first-time user. Compare reported “Opened locally”; no upload or playback action was taken. A follow-up “Discard session” click timed out in browser control before a confirmation state could be read; no deletion was verified or claimed. No real animal or participant data was made; no research admission, deployment or model run occurred. The active five-track goal remains open; next acceptance is the planned uncoached outside-user walkthrough and named-device save/reopen test, while discard handling and protocol/consent/hosting gates remain open.

## October 25 — add a context-aware vervet study to the evidence library

**Question:** what does the vervet alarm-call literature teach about choosing an initial animal question and designing observations that do not mistake a signal for a translated word?

**Evidence:** reviewed the Wiley abstract and German summary for the 1980 developmental study, the ScienceDirect abstract for the longer 1980b playback paper, the 1980a Science PubMed abstract, selected full-text sections of Fischer (2020), and the full Springer article for Deshpande et al. (2023). The 2023 article reports eight adult female subjects from one group and 16 within-subject trials. Lower speaker-directed looking and vigilance were reported when a male alarm bark played during an ongoing intergroup encounter than when no encounter was underway; no predator-specific escape response occurred. The paper's Results and Discussion contradict each other on the direction of the startle effect, so this measure is unresolved. The article declares CC BY 4.0 for the paper and links data/code via OSF; those artifacts and their separate rights/privacy terms were not inspected.

**Change and decision:** updated the vervet note and source/resource catalog, and added the KwaZulu-Natal study region alongside Amboseli. The atlas deliberately omits the exact wildlife-site coordinates printed in the paper. Vervet literature is a useful context/receiver-response methods case, not a ready open benchmark or first phone cohort. Added this comparison to `docs/FIRST_DATASET_DECISION.md`; the public app remains species-neutral, and no animal cohort or model was selected.

**Validation and limits:** 172 Python tests passed with one intentional external-sample skip; all 122 Node tests passed; the collection validator accepted 223 resources. The local noindex build produced 138 pages plus 404; the checker validated 139 HTML documents and 7,120 links. The generated vervet route showed the updated title, 2023 citation, bounded findings and next-reading note. The owner-local SQLite catalog imported 757 public metadata records; an immediate repeat changed zero. `git diff --check` passed. No data, code, audio or model was acquired or run; no hosted database, external contact or deployment was involved. This is a source-led editorial and atlas update, not independent scientific review, a new experiment or evidence of animal communication.

## October 25 — map HyenaSET at country scale

**Question and evidence:** can the atlas represent HyenaSET's study geography without exposing wildlife locations? The primary [bioRxiv v2 abstract](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.14.732108v2) identifies collar recordings from 19 spotted hyenas in Maasai Mara National Reserve, Kenya. ## October 25 — deepen the parakeet dataset review and add coarse study geography

**Research decision:** targeted publisher-indexed passages in Eberhard et al. describe a study of loud contact calls, not the full vocal repertoire. The paper reports acoustic differences among five island populations and mainland Venezuela, and explicitly requires receiver experiments before interpreting those patterns as response differences. It does not establish call meaning. Mainland caller units may be groups, and the data’s session/group structure remains unverified.

**Provenance and rights:** the current Dryad API record identifies dataset 77790, version 4 / version ID 147704, CC0-1.0 at the dataset-record level, publication date 2021-11-01, and eight files totaling 57,091,189 bytes. The upstream SHA-256 manifest is preserved at `research/manifests/brown-throated-parakeet-dryad-v4.json`; its values are not locally verified because no payload was downloaded. The README API download returned HTTP 401, and its official record-page download link returned HTTP 403. No alternate route was attempted. Article-specific reuse rights remain unverified; the paper and dataset are separate collection resources. The manifest contains no executable code file, although the paper describes a custom MATLAB routine.

**Atlas change:** the primary paper says its island coordinates are study-area centers and that individual recording sites are in the separate Dryad files. The candidate atlas maps those five centers rounded to one decimal and uses a generic Venezuela country reference; it does not show individual site or animal points. The local projection contains 108 notes, 41 mapped across 44 places, 42 pending and 25 not applicable, with fingerprint `a11f98ba1a2f85d6d50509f5f05a6a6fc142afb28edb7b2e2f6e8b1afd12f804`.

## October 25 — review cross-taxon Perch 2.0 transfer

**Evidence reviewed:** the full arXiv v1 HTML paper by Burns et al. (2025), its methods, dataset descriptions, results and discussion, plus the official Google Research summary. The study uses Perch 2.0 embeddings with few-shot linear probes for marine species/ecotype and broad sound classification. It reports strong transfer results, making frozen acoustic embeddings a plausible candidate baseline for a narrow rights-cleared task.

**Decision:** the result supports acoustic feature transfer, not animal-language translation. The paper describes random recording-level few-shot splits; it does not establish site-, deployment-, session- or individual-held-out evaluation. It also says evaluation tasks were among Perch 2.0's development validation sources, while separately describing DCLDE as unseen; exact model-selection overlap remains unresolved. The article notes around a dozen mainly above-water cetacean examples in Perch 2.0 training. No audio, code, dataset or model was acquired or run. Added source 450, a public evidence-note update, and a metadata-only article resource; underlying data/model rights remain separate. AutoResearch stays **NOT READY**.

## October 26 — refresh NLnet funding fit after policy 2.0

**Evidence:** reviewed NLnet's current [GenAI policy v2.0](https://nlnet.nl/foundation/policies/generativeAI/2.0/), its [FAQ](https://nlnet.nl/foundation/policies/generativeAI/faq/), its [October 20 applicability notice](https://nlnet.nl/news/2026/20261020-AI-policy.html), and the [CodeSupply eligibility rules](https://nlnet.nl/codesupply/eligibility/). NLnet says applications cannot be written using GenAI; funded deliverables cannot use GenAI to produce code, design or documentation; significant pre-existing AI-generated code must be disclosed and is assessed. The new policy applies to both open November 3 calls. CodeSupply requires a strong European dimension (normally at least 60% of funds to Horizon Europe-eligible team members, with an exceptional-quality/unique-expertise route for others) and a clear work-package link to its supply-chain metadata scope.

**Decision:** mark CodeSupply “Open · not recommended this cycle.” Talk2Nature has no validated package-metadata need, no established European team dimension, and no completed inventory of its AI-assisted codebase for disclosure. This is a readiness recommendation, not a legal eligibility ruling. Restack remains out of scope because the project has no evidence of the one-million-active-user threshold or a stack-relevant work package. No proposal was generated, drafted, submitted or discussed with NLnet.

**Change and next step:** added the current policy, FAQ and applicability notice as source records; updated both opportunity cards and their links; added a regression check for the current policy and status. Continue the zero-command product work on its own evidence gate: render and inspect the candidate when browser access is available, then use the existing physical-phone and authorized uncoached-user checks. This local candidate remains unpushed and undeployed. The five-track goal remains active; AutoResearch, first-benchmark readiness and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

## October 26 — record OS4LS as a closed-call readiness signal

**Question:** is the Open Source for Science Fund's 2026 Open Source for the Life Sciences call a realistic funding route for Talk2Nature now, or a future readiness target?

**Evidence reviewed:** the Fund's official funding index lists OS4LS 2026 as closed and no later call. The official call page says LOI submissions are closed; LOIs were due June 8, full proposals July 21, and notifications were scheduled for October 2026. It offered up to $250,000 for Track 1 or $1,000,000 for Track 2 over two years. Eligibility requires an organization or fiscal sponsor; foreign organizations may apply subject to applicable compliance, but individuals cannot receive grants. The RFA requires mature software and demonstrated life-sciences adoption. It names early-stage prototypes without broader use and proposals for repository/database hosting, model development or new dataset generation as unlikely or out of scope.

**Change:** added the two official fund pages as sources and the closed OS4LS call to the public opportunity catalog. The record separates thematic relevance (open research software and reproducible data/model workflows) from present readiness (no verified external life-sciences adoption, no selected technical bottleneck, and no confirmed applicant organization). It states that later calls may have different terms.

**Validation:** added focused regression coverage for status, source links, deadlines and fit limits. All 178 Python tests passed with one intentional skip; all 131 Node tests passed. The local noindex build checked 143 HTML files and 7,417 links; the explicit-origin public build checked 143 HTML files, 7,560 links and all 142 public routes. `git diff --check` passed.

**Decision and limits:** no application, mailing-list subscription or contact was made. This is not an active funding offer, does not establish an eligible project applicant, and should not trigger application work until a later call is officially listed and the readiness gates are stronger. No project coding or data-generation plan was proposed for this fund.
