## October 26 — add a bounded cockatiel receiver-response reference while keeping zero-command gates open

**Question:** can a controlled parrot study improve Talk2Nature's measurement design without encouraging an unsafe app behavior or a general translation claim?

**Evidence reviewed:** the full primary [PLOS ONE article](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0205314), including its ethics statement, methods, results, discussion and supporting-information manifest. The study tested ten captive cockatiels with partner distress calls, familiar non-partner calls and white noise on separate days. During playback, partner and non-partner distress calls each increased call counts relative to white noise, but partner versus non-partner call counts did not differ (p=.37). Partner-versus-nonpartner movement was marginal (p=.07), and one crest-posture comparison was p=.05. The partner-call loudspeaker-distance comparison could not be tested because no bird spent more than 15 seconds near the speaker. The article page states CC BY 4.0 for the article and lists S1 Data as an XLSX. The XLSX was not downloaded or inspected; its file-specific reuse terms were not separately verified.

**Change:** added one source-grounded research note, two source records and separate paper/data resource entries. The article is a controlled receiver-response methods comparator, not a translation result, ML benchmark or claim about subjective animal states. The paper describes brief glove-associated restraint to elicit distress calls and controlled playback; those methods are explicitly excluded from the passive companion-app path. The S1 Data workbook remains metadata-only with unresolved rights. Budgerigars remain the provisional household-app cohort; cockatiels are a separate option only if access and independent avian review support it. Updated the first-animal plan, research backlog and active goal.

**Validation:** all 183 Python tests passed with one intentional skip; all 131 Node tests passed. The local noindex build produced 143 pages plus 404; the site checker validated 144 HTML files and 7,472 links. The explicit-origin public candidate used `https://metivity.github.io/talk2nature`, built 143 pages plus 404 and passed the public checker with 144 HTML files, 7,617 links and 143 reachable public pages. `git diff --check` and JSON parsing passed. These are maintainer-run build and content checks, not a deployment, independent reproduction, expert review or animal-communication result.

**Zero-command status and next decision:** the user asked to continue the zero-command path. The current browser-control call timed out before rendered inspection, so this milestone makes no UI or phone-usability claim and changes no app interface. Keep the existing gates open: inspect the current flow at 320, 390 and desktop widths when browser control recovers, test on a named physical phone, and complete the authorized uncoached-user walkthrough. No tester was contacted and no animal recording was made or acquired. Keep the overall five-track goal active; AutoResearch and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

## October 27 continuation — deepen the great-tit receiver-response record

**Question:** what does the Carolina-wren playback study actually measure, what alternative explanation limits its sequence claim, and what separate data asset does it expose?

**Evidence reviewed:** publisher-indexed primary article sections (Introduction, Methods, Results, Discussion, Ethical Note, Data Availability and Supplementary Material listing) plus the [University of Western Australia article record](https://research-repository.uwa.edu.au/en/publications/mobbing-sequences-of-american-wrens-elicit-mobbing-responses-in-e). The direct ScienceDirect page still returned HTTP 403, so no article file, figures or supplement bytes were opened.

**Finding:** the article reports 96 wild great tits, one playback each across six treatments. Call playback increased approach relative to controls, but call treatments did not differ significantly. Wing-flicking was more likely after FME–D sequences than after D-only calls or controls; the interspecies sequence comparison was nonsignificant and imprecise. The authors identify unmatched acoustic content/duty cycle as an alternative explanation: the FME–D sequence contains more material than the D-only playback, with no matched D-only condition. Carolina-wren stimuli were built from four source birds but only two tracks per treatment; no reversed-order Carolina-wren sequence was tested. The authors' ethics statement is recorded as their account, not a project protocol or independent welfare review.

**Data and rights decision:** the publisher says study data are in Supplementary Material and lists one 985-byte text file. Added source record 464 and a separate metadata-only dataset resource; its filename, bytes, contents and file-specific terms remain unknown. The UWA record links the paper under CC BY 4.0; that does not clear the supplementary file's exact terms or the separate Macaulay Library/Xeno-Canto stimulus recordings. Nothing was acquired, opened, played or analyzed. The article note and prioritized backlog now include these limits. Keep the app passive; do not treat receiver behavior as translation.

**Next:** the physical-phone save/reopen gate and authorized newcomer walkthrough remain open. Continue with accessible primary-source review or reproducibility improvements while waiting for device evidence. The five-track goal stays active; AutoResearch and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

## October 27 — clarify how to save a typed observation

**Finding:** in a synthetic browser walk-through, a typed note was not part of the saved moment until the user also pressed “Mark an observation.” The Station warned about the draft only after Stop, so a first-time user could reasonably expect “Save this moment” to include it.

**Change:** added plain-language guidance before capture explaining that the user should press “Mark an observation” while recording, and that unmarked text remains on the page and is not downloaded. The post-stop warning now says the note was not added and can be copied elsewhere. The helper is hidden during review when its instruction is no longer actionable; the draft warning remains visible and is linked to the textarea for assistive technology. Added UI and copy regression checks.

**Validation:** all 135 Node tests passed; all 185 Python tests passed with one intentional skip. A fresh local noindex build generated 143 pages plus 404; the site checker validated 144 HTML documents and 7,484 links; `git diff --check` passed. Headless desktop Chrome at 390×844 emulated pixels completed the synthetic flow from the Companion home through note entry and explicit timeline marking, Stop, ZIP download, confirmation, and reopening that exact ZIP in Compare; the saved note appeared under “Recordings & observations.” An unmarked draft produced the updated warning. At 320×740, the note instructions and “Mark an observation” control were visible together after scrolling, with no horizontal overflow. Both were maintainer-run synthetic checks: zero microphone API calls, external requests, browser errors or animal data.

**Limits and next:** no physical phone, real recording, outside-user study, assistive-technology review or biological validation was part of this check. The named-phone save/reopen gate and authorized uncoached-newcomer walkthrough remain open; no tester was contacted. The five-track goal remains active; AutoResearch and real-data admission stay **NOT READY**.

## October 27 funding review — Israel Innovation Authority Pre-Seed

**Question:** is the November 9 call a realistic funding route for Talk2Nature at its current documented readiness?

**Official evidence:** rechecked the [IIA Pre-Seed track page](https://innovationisrael.org.il/programs/preseed/) on October 27. It lists the next cutoff as November 9, 2026 at 12:00 Israel time. The page describes a Pre-Seed round for an Israeli-incorporated company whose main activity is commercial R&D; total prior funding may not exceed NIS 3 million at the program's stated cutoff, prior-calendar-year revenue may not exceed NIS 300,000, at least 75% of a requested round of up to NIS 6 million must be R&D, and the application must include signed evidence of a recognized private-investor commitment worth at least 15% of that round. The IIA portion is 60% of the eligible round up to NIS 2 million. This is a co-investment grant attached to a company financing round, not a stand-alone grant for an open-source repository.

**Readiness decision:** the project records do not document an applying Israeli company, a signed investor commitment, the company's funding/revenue thresholds, or a specific R&D round and budget. The project's open-source/IP treatment under the IIA materials is separately unresolved. Recommendation: **no-go for this call at current documented readiness**; no application draft or submission was made. Reconsider only after those applicant and investment facts are confirmed and the IIA's treatment of pre-existing and funded open-source assets is clarified in writing. Updated the opportunity and source records without adding any private applicant information.

**Next:** retain the deadline and no-go rationale as a dated opportunity record; prioritize other grants only when their actual applicant and consortium requirements match the project's current capacity. The five-track goal remains active. This funding review does not change the open physical-phone/newcomer gates or the **NOT READY** status for AutoResearch and real-data admission.

## October 27 — verify and structurally inspect cockatiel S1 Data

**Question:** can the paper's supporting spreadsheet contribute a bounded, rights-clear record about observable receiver responses, and does it contain data suited to an acoustic model?

**Evidence:** the primary PLOS ONE page links S1 Data as an XLSX. The official PLOS Figshare API item 7184276 v1 declares CC BY 4.0 and lists file 13219823 (`pone.0205314.s001.xlsx`), 34,661 bytes, with matching supplied/computed MD5 `10f1a76b6fabb6542982817d8d19371b`. A live verified-TLS read immediately before acquisition confirmed item DOI, version, license, exact file ID/name, size, MD5 and download route.

**Change:** the bounded collector gained the PLOS-specific storage prefix observed on the normal Figshare redirect. It allows only the pinned S3 host/path/file ID/name and a transient signed query; the receipt does not retain the signed URL. A synthetic regression test rejects a different tenant prefix, file ID, filename, missing signature or unexpected redirect. The exact 34,661-byte workbook was acquired into ignored `data/external/`; the receipt verifies provider MD5 and local SHA-256, and keeps `training_admitted=false`. Added `scripts/inspect_cockatiel_s1.py` and `research/cockatiel_s1_report.json` for aggregate-only structural review.

**Finding:** the workbook has a Data sheet with 210 behavioral windows and 20 columns, plus a Legend sheet. Ten tested cockatiels have 21 repeated windows each: 90 playback, 90 post-playback and 30 pre-stimulus rows. The three social conditions each have 70 rows. These are repeated observations nested within ten animals, not 210 independent subjects; the workbook has behavior-coded outcomes but no audio. No raw rows or animal names are emitted. No GLMM or inferential result was reproduced.

**Validation:** Figshare manifest assertions and the bounded collector passed; receipt checks confirmed exact size, MD5 and SHA-256 with no signed query retained. All 28 collection tests and both new synthetic workbook-audit tests pass; rerunning the checksum-pinned audit reports 210 rows and ten birds with `training_admitted=false`. The full Python suite passes (188 tests, one intentional skip), all 137 Node tests pass, and the 241-entry resource catalog validates. A fresh local build produced 143 pages plus 404 and passed 7,484 link checks; an explicit-origin public candidate passed the publication checks for 143 routes and 7,628 links. `git diff --check` passes. These are maintainer checks, not independent scientific reproduction.

**Limits and next decision:** this is a small methods/reproducibility reference, not an acoustic dataset, translation benchmark, research admission or endorsement of the study's distress-call elicitation/playback protocol. No audio, video, code or model was acquired or run; no independent avian or statistics reviewer has assessed the findings. Keep the sample local, out of training and private server storage. The provisional budgerigar household cohort remains unchanged. Continue toward the named-phone workflow and authorized newcomer gate; keep AutoResearch and real-data admission **NOT READY**.

## October 27 — reproduce the monk-parakeet selection-table exclusion

**Question:** can the 2023 study's 14-row cross-scale exclusion be checked directly against its selection CSV without retrieving audio or treating the table as a model dataset?

**Rights and provenance:** a live verified-TLS read of the official Figshare v2 API and item page confirmed DOI `10.6084/m9.figshare.22582099.v2`, item-level CC BY 4.0, and file 40086154 at 440,873 bytes with provider MD5 `3296a9792c395fbad6d9bfa8e13a482f`. The bounded collector retrieved only that CSV into ignored `data/external/monk-parakeet-identity-figshare-2023/`; the receipt records matching provider MD5 and local SHA-256 `920a34a910b43a151eab8f2361ee2d445281d7bea84329ecf1a02b98a630f169`. No audio or other Figshare file was retrieved.

**Implementation and result:** added `scripts/inspect_monk_parakeet_selection_table.py` and `research/monk_parakeet_selection_audit.json`. The audit verifies the current catalog and acquisition receipt before reading the CSV. It found 1,596 rows (229 Individual, 1,367 Site); 31 Site rows include the `_site_scale` marker, and 14 have a Bird_ID also present in Individual rows. Removing those 14 yields 1,582, matching the paper's analysis count and the selection rule in the pinned MIT R source. The report emits aggregate counts and hashes only; identifiers, filenames, locations, coordinates and free-text fields are excluded. Two synthetic tests cover the filter, checksum failure and non-disclosure.

**Validation and limits:** the focused audit tests and full repository suites pass: 191 Python tests (one intentional skip) and 137 Node tests. Collection validation accepts 241 records; the bounded plan remains offline and limited to 440,873 bytes. The explicit-origin public build produced 143 routes, 144 HTML documents including 404, and passed 7,629 link checks. The built resource page uses the human Figshare landing page, and a publication-boundary scan found no acquired sample path or data file in `dist/`. This is CSV-level reconciliation, not execution of the R source or extended RDS, audio/file alignment, model reproduction, or a behavior/meaning result. The linked audio archive remains unacquired; the separate 2020 GitHub selection-table rights remain unresolved. Training admission stays false; AutoResearch and the first benchmark remain **NOT READY**. Next: review file-level recording provenance and leakage-safe grouping with a qualified bioacoustics reviewer before any audio or model work.

The source and resource catalogs and bat-context note now preserve this as metadata-only. ## October 28 — add an Oriental magpie-robin urban-acoustics reference

The README also identifies coordinate fields, so the atlas uses only a coarse Kathmandu Valley marker; no CSV or precise location was acquired or published. This is a methods comparator, not an audio/communication benchmark; the first-dataset recommendation is unchanged.

## October 28 — make the phone save step easier to recover

**Evidence:** WebKit's report [#275288](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=275288) documents that, in an iOS 17 home-screen web-app case, a generated download could open in a preview and require an additional save action. WebKit marked the issue moved to Safari's UI; this old report does not establish current iOS behavior or Talk2Nature's ZIP behavior. Apple's iPhone guide documents finding internet downloads in Files → Browse → Downloads ([source](https://support.apple.com/en-asia/guide/iphone/iphc9cd7266c/26/ios/26)).

**Change:** revised Station's primary post-save message to explain: check Downloads; if a preview opens, choose Share → Save to Files (or the device's save option), return to Talk2Nature, confirm saving, then open Compare. Changed the acknowledgement label from “I found my downloaded moment” to “I saved the file.” The file remains created locally by the existing download mechanism; no share API, upload or persistent browser storage was added.

The CUA screen attempt timed out and device enumeration returned no attached physical phone; Android Debug Bridge is unavailable. The five-track goal remains active; AutoResearch and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

**Checks:** all 194 Python tests passed (one intentional skip), and all 140 Node tests passed. The local noindex build and site checker passed (145 HTML documents, 7,565 links). Playwright WebKit 26.0 at a 390 × 844 CSS-pixel viewport completed the full synthetic home-to-Compare flow: it generated an 80,915-byte ZIP, reopened the exact file, reported “Opened locally,” kept document/body width at 390 pixels, made zero microphone calls and raised no page errors. This was desktop WebKit viewport emulation with programmatic file selection, not a physical phone, iOS Safari, installed app, native file picker or independent user.

## October 28 — refresh NLnet Restack scope and funding-policy decision

**Question:** does the active November 3 Restack call present a suitable funding path for Talk2Nature, and is the prior one-million-user threshold still stated?

**Evidence reviewed:** reopened NLnet's official [Restack program page](https://nlnet.nl/restack/), [eligibility page](https://nlnet.nl/restack/eligibility/), [FAQ](https://nlnet.nl/restack/faq/), [application form](https://nlnet.nl/propose/), [policy 2.0](https://nlnet.nl/foundation/policies/generativeAI/2.0/) and [October 20 applicability notice](https://nlnet.nl/news/2026/20261020-AI-policy.html). The call page states November 3, 2026 at noon CET and €5,000–€50,000 first proposals. The current Restack program and eligibility pages do not contain the previously recorded one-million-user AI exception; no archived page was reviewed, so no page-change history is asserted. Current eligibility instead requires a strong European dimension; the usual route gives at least 60% of funds to team members from Horizon Europe-eligible countries. The [Commission's 2026–2027 General Annexes](https://ec.europa.eu/info/funding-tenders/opportunities/docs/2021-2027/horizon/wp-call/2026-2027/wp-15-general-annexes_horizon-2026-2027_en.pdf) list Israel among associated countries. This does not establish the project owner's residence, team composition or proposed funding split. The FAQ permits an individual or informal organisation to apply without prior incorporation.

**Decision and limits:** policy 2.0 applies to this call: GenAI may not write proposals or produce funded project artifacts; read-only use is permitted, and significant pre-existing AI-generated code must be disclosed. Talk2Nature has no defined open-internet-stack deliverable or validated user need, and its AI-assisted codebase has not been inventoried for that disclosure. Do not prepare or submit for this cycle. This is a project-fit/readiness decision, not a formal legal-eligibility ruling. No application or outreach occurred. Updated sources 150, 151, 456 and 458, added current sources 475–476, and revised the opportunity record. Continue the five-track goal; AutoResearch and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

**Future watch:** the current [ELFA consortium page](https://elfaconsortium.eu/) says subgranting is expected to open January 1, 2027, but publishes no amount, eligibility, application guide or deadline. NLnet's [ELFA status page](https://nlnet.nl/ELFA/) says it withdrew as operator and that the call was postponed for another organization to run. Added metadata-only sources 477–478 and a planned-call watch entry. A local-first animal-observation workflow may be adjacent, but no current terms establish fit; no contact or partnership is claimed.

## October 28 — preserve native observation notes until confirmed

**Question:** does Android Station clear a human-entered note only after the encrypted local session confirms it was persisted?

**Finding and change:** source review found `MainActivity.addObservationNote()` cleared the text and displayed a success toast immediately after `startService()`. `CaptureService.addNote()` swallowed failures, so a stopped service, storage error or delivery failure could silently lose the note. The service now returns success only after `SessionStore.addMarker()` completes; failures produce a negative result. The activity waits for that result, clears only the exact submitted text after success, leaves text entered during the request intact, and preserves visible text with an error state after failure or an unconfirmed timeout.

**Validation:** the Java core test covers confirmed clearing, failed persistence and edits made during submission. Node source-level regressions verify the activity waits for the result and the service acknowledges only after the local write. The native core checks passed; the two native Node handoff/note tests passed; all 197 Python tests passed with one intentional skip; all 142 Node tests passed; the noindex build produced 145 pages plus 404, and the site checker validated 146 HTML documents and 7,644 links. Public checks [#40657265969](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/40657265969) passed repository, browser-tool, Android and preview-site jobs; the Android job assembled debug and release APKs and verified release permission/network/debug boundaries. This confirms compilation and package boundaries, not runtime delivery of the new result callback. The local environment has no Android SDK, and no emulator interaction with this new path, physical-phone use or newcomer comprehension occurred. PR [#537](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/537) merged as [`98c4259`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/98c425964c49abac9b61f3e6a78c281a92a3ad82); no Pages deployment was needed because public site assets did not change. No audio was recorded, uploaded or admitted as research data. The project goal remains active; AutoResearch and real-data admission remain **NOT READY**.

## October 28 — guard Android post-session playback for human review

**Finding:** the native app only rendered its clip controls after capture stopped, but its “Play clip” control could start `AudioTrack` without a nearby warning. The browser Station already tells reviewers to use headphones away from animals. This mismatch left the native path open to accidental animal-directed playback.

**Change:** renamed the control “Review audio” and placed playback behind a confirmation that says not to play recordings to animals and to use headphones or review away from the animal. Cancel leaves the clip unplayed; the positive action is the only route to `playEvent`. This is a human safeguard, not headphone-route enforcement or proof of acoustic isolation.

## October 28 — add a bounded humpback caller-attribution case study

Added a source-linked note for Shearer et al. (2026), with separate article, dataset and code metadata records (source IDs 486–488). The full open-access article was reviewed along with the institutional dataset description and versioned deposit metadata. It reports that animal-borne accelerometer signals rarely matched known focal calls and that some high-amplitude non-focal calls appeared on tags, including calls present on two tags. The note separates the paper abstract's 3% overall summary from the 5.1% sensitivity calculated for its 1,090-call strongest-validation subset. It does not infer phone-camera or handheld-sensor performance, receiver understanding or call meaning.

The study region is mapped only to a one-decimal Stellwagen Bank sanctuary locator derived from NOAA's boundary reference; no whale tracks, tag positions or deployment coordinates are published. The paper and data/code deposit are recorded separately. The deposit's CC BY 4.0 is only a record-level declaration; no distinct MATLAB software/dependency license was verified. No audio, sensor data, code, model weights or third-party files were acquired or run; this is not a Talk2Nature benchmark. The research atlas now has 45 mapped evidence records. These metadata-only additions do not change the zero-command physical-phone gate or authorize real-data intake.

**Architecture follow-through:** updated `docs/DISCOVERY_PLAN.md` to carry this as a caution, not a phone-performance result. Future audio/video studies should preserve unknown caller labels, record clock uncertainty, compare background-only, audio-only, visual-observation-only and combined baselines, and group evaluation by animal and session. No camera feature, model, real-data intake or benchmark was added.

## October 28 — align browser review with zero-command animal boundary

## October 28 — confirm immediately before browser playback

**Finding:** the preceding Station safeguard asked for headphones when a reviewer loaded a clip, then exposed native audio controls. A delay before pressing Play could make that confirmation stale. The review action has been split into silent local loading and a separate play action.

**Change:** Station's native audio controls are removed. “Load for review” creates a local object URL in a hidden player and focuses a custom Play/Pause button; it does not invoke playback. Every Play or replay asks the reviewer immediately beforehand to use headphones or move away. Cancel does not call `play()`; Pause stops the clip. Sound desk now pauses its native review player when hidden or leaving the page; Compare already paused on background and cleared on page exit. Their manual controls and headphone warnings remain; the per-play confirmation applies only to Station, and exported files can still be played externally.

**Verification:** focused Station UI tests passed (7/7) and Sound desk UI tests passed (13/13); all 147 Node tests passed; all 199 Python tests passed with one intentional skip; the local noindex build produced 146 pages plus 404; `scripts/check_site.py` validated 147 HTML documents and 7,744 links. The fixtures use invented tones only. Two in-app browser state checks timed out, so no rendered mobile/browser interaction or physical-device playback test is claimed. `adb` is unavailable; the listed iOS simulators are unrelated QA devices and shut down. A confirmation cannot verify headphones, distance or animal audibility. Nothing was pushed or deployed.

**Next:** inspect the rendered Station control when browser access is available; perform the zero-command save/reopen and no-stimulus task on a named physical phone. Then decide whether to apply the same custom playback gate to Listen and Compare. Keep AutoResearch and real-data admission **NOT READY**.

## October 28 — align human playback controls across Listen and Compare

**Finding:** Station already required a fresh headphone/distance confirmation at playback time, but Listen and Compare still exposed native media controls that could be replayed after an older warning. That left three different safety behaviors across the review tools.

**Change:** added a shared local review controller to Listen and Compare. It provides Play/Pause, seek and elapsed-time display; loading and scrubbing do not play audio. Every playback start, including a replay after Pause, Stop or backgrounding, asks for an immediate confirmation. Cancel never calls `play()`. Playback pauses when the page is hidden or left, and the player stays separate from microphone capture. Added responsive styles and included the controller and styles in the exact app offline allowlist. Station continues using its own separately reviewed control.

## October 28 — screen current Mozilla funding opportunities

**Question:** which live Mozilla route, if any, merits founder attention for this open-science project?

**Evidence:** reviewed official Mozilla sources 489–494: the 2027–2028 Fellowship call page, full RFP, grant eligibility guide and FAQ; and the 2026 Human-Scale AI Incubator call and FAQ. The Fellowship offers an individual Independent Fellow a $100,000 stipend and $25,000 project budget for twelve months, with its Letter of Intent due December 17, 2026. Its RFP specifically mentions community environmental sensing for biodiversity and public infrastructure. Applicants must be legally able to receive Mozilla funds and show charitable purpose/public benefit; individual eligibility is not confirmed. The RFP limits AI to supportive application assistance, with answers in the applicant’s own voice.

**Decision:** record the Fellowship as a conditional thematic lead and the Incubator as not recommended on present evidence. Incubator terms require AI to be core, working technology already used by its intended community, and early product-community-fit evidence. Talk2Nature has no validated animal-audio model or documented independent adoption signal. Added both opportunities to the public catalog and a private question-led prep note at ignored `funding/mozilla-fellowship-2027.md`; no personal application details were inferred.

**Limits and next:** no applicant location, USD payment route, legal eligibility, charitable-purpose determination, personal availability, target-community evidence or founder narrative was verified. The candidate fit does not establish eligibility or likelihood of selection. The applicant must decide whether to apply individually, confirm these facts and write any Letter of Intent personally. No application was opened, submitted or sent; no organization was contacted. Recheck the official call before acting. The five-track goal remains active.

## October 29 — trace the Wytham model-assisted labels to the pinned code release

**Question:** does the 2025 reproducibility release expose the source and method behind the 31 model-assisted repertoire identities, and does it provide their mapping?

**Sources reviewed:** the official Zenodo API version record 10.5281/zenodo.12800736 (open access, record-level CC BY 4.0, one 303,621-byte ZIP); the GitHub v1.0.0 tag resolved to commit 96a75e8869edef36a77632b3c85f019bcd027053; its README.md, LICENSE.md, .gitattributes, two CSV LFS pointer texts and selected sections of scripts/setup/0.1-assign-ids.R. The code license is MIT. The README instructs readers to obtain the data separately from OSF, but the link in this pinned revision is empty. CSVs are tracked by Git LFS; the inspected paths expose pointer text, not data values.

**Finding:** the script calculates pairwise distances from feature vectors, filters candidate links to different years and nest locations under 100 m apart with feature distance below 0.9, then propagates a known bird ID to an otherwise unlabelled record. This confirms a model-assisted label path; the labels are not independent ground truth for a benchmark derived from overlapping features. The release does not reveal the exact 31-repertoire membership or its overlap with Gallego et al.'s 2026 subset.

Added separate Zenodo and pinned GitHub source records and a metadata-only code resource; updated the Wytham evidence note and first-dataset decision. No archive, LFS object, audio or model was acquired; no script was executed, no assignment reproduced and no result inferred. The benchmark remains **NOT READY**. Only pursue further Wytham data access if an independent avian-bioacoustics/evaluation review identifies a distinct task and the exact OSF object and file-level rights can be established.

**Decision and goal update (October 29):** keep the project zero-command and passive. Wytham remains a possible song-structure methods resource, not a communication benchmark: its prior identity assignments are model-assisted, and it has no receiver-response or meaning labels. The next research deliverable is a task-specific species comparison using independent behavioral/receiver-response labels, exact accessible files and component-level rights, leakage-safe individual/site splits and qualified review capacity. Keep the five-track goal active; do not admit real data or claim translation.

## October 29 — add MarmAudio to the evidence library and defer benchmark selection

**Question:** does the newly reviewed marmoset corpus provide a credible starting point for Talk2Nature's first communication or machine-learning benchmark?

**Sources reviewed:** Lamothe et al. (2025), *A large annotated dataset of vocalizations by common marmosets*, full open-access article sections on recording design, segmentation/labeling, data records, technical validation and limitations; current Zenodo record v7 (`17191682`) page and verified-TLS API metadata; and the public MarmAudio GitHub README and pinned `BSD-3-Clause` license at commit `5c65268f2734f0abb4e26a83877cedbc4e9c8330`. Zenodo declares record-level `CC BY 4.0`; the paper is separately `CC BY-NC-ND 4.0`. The current nine-file record totals 60,366,144,089 bytes. The paper cites an earlier version DOI (`15017207`); the current record is part of concept `10997563`. No audio, annotations, metadata PDF, code, dependencies or model weights were downloaded or run.

**Finding and decision:** the authors report 871,044 segmented vocalizations across 253 hours, but only about 215,000 clips were assigned one of six specific call types. The pipeline is model-assisted, and four experts re-checked a 700-clip sample with model predictions visible. The authors explicitly say sender identity and production context are absent. The validation prose reports 9.43% average error while its table reports 8.71%; reconcile before reproducing. This is a valuable call-type methods corpus, not a caller-held-out, behavior/context, receiver-response or meaning dataset. Added it as a metadata-only research note/resource, updated the candidate matrix and independent-review packet, and recorded a cross-candidate decision to defer benchmark selection. The bat, zebra-finch, honeybee and household-parrot paths each retain distinct evidence or review gaps; no task is authorized for data/model execution.

## October 29 — reuse the resource directory's rendered search text

**Question:** can resource search become less work per keystroke without shrinking or hiding the 284 detailed records that document rights, limitations and research decisions?

**Baseline:** a verified-TLS GET of the live `/research/resources/` page returned 962,166 bytes uncompressed. With `Accept-Encoding: gzip, br`, the server selected gzip and transferred 190,937 bytes before the change. Parsing found 284 server-rendered resource cards, each with detailed research and rights notes, and zero `data-search` attributes. The payload is primarily the visible/crawlable catalog itself, so further byte trimming would remove useful source context. Search previously called `textContent.toLocaleLowerCase()` for each card on every input/filter update.

**Change:** cache one lowercased copy of each card's existing rendered text at initialization and reuse it in subsequent search/filter passes. Do not add redundant HTML search fields or omit any resource, rights statement, citation, limitation or review note. The regression verifies full-text querying remains intact and `textContent` is read once per card across repeated search/filter edits.

**Verification:** full Python suite passed 237 tests with one intentional skip; full Node suite passed 163 tests. The local noindex build and site checker accepted 155 HTML documents and 10,586 links. Chrome 156 evaluated the built page at 390 and 320 CSS-pixel viewports: all 284 records loaded, “mushroom” found one, “bird” with the dataset filter found 45, and document/body widths matched the viewport at both widths. This is local desktop-browser emulation, not physical-phone or assistive-technology evidence. Focused `tests/resources.test.mjs` passed after the final full-suite run; the only subsequent change to that file added a case-insensitive full-detail query assertion, and it passed 2/2.

**Release and live readback:** PR [#587](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/587) merged as [`31a6617`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/31a6617acadf6721d4aee9a173990e80c56ccf7a). Pages run [#40863656912](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/40863656912) passed all build, route-preservation, SEO-boundary and live-route checks. After release, a verified-TLS GET returned HTTP 200 and 962,166 uncompressed bytes; a request advertising gzip and Brotli received gzip at 190,938 bytes. The published `resources.js?v=4f8efdc9f979b467` was 1,804 bytes and byte-matched the merged source (SHA-256 `22c7576729cbec392c1a92038ea519f643fb406bf96c3db846118096708999a3`). Headless Chrome 156 on the live route found all 284 records, one “mushroom” result, 45 for “bird” plus the dataset filter, and matching document/body viewport widths at 390 and 320 CSS pixels. This is live desktop-browser emulation, not a physical-phone, assistive-technology or independent-user evaluation; no search-latency benchmark was run.

**Decision and next:** the optimization preserves all published catalog content and reduces repeated DOM text extraction. No external data, media, models or animal recordings were used. Pages run [#40892470302](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/40892470302) passed its public build, test, route-preservation, publication-boundary and live-route checks. The public build preserved all 154 routes; the site checker accepted 155 HTML documents and 10,743 links.

A fresh TLS read with cache bypass returned HTTP 200 for `/app/` (14,125 bytes). The animal-facing sentence appeared before the first action. This confirms the deployed markup and copy, not device behavior, screen-reader accessibility or newcomer comprehension. The named-device and independent-user gates remain open; the five-track goal remains active.
